Title: The Confession or Declaration of the Ministers or Pastors, which in the United Provinces are called Remonstrants, or Arminians, concerning the chief Points of Christian Religion. In XXV Chapters. Twice Printed in Dutch, Twice in Latine, and now for its great worth Carefully Translated into English
Creator: The Remonstrant ministers of the United Provinces; the Confession of 1621 was written by Simon Episcopius (the host catalogue lists Episcopius and Johannes Wtenbogaert as creators of the 1621 Dutch printing; the English title page names no author); English translator not named
Publisher: London: printed for Francis Smith at the Elephant and Castle in Cornhill near the Royal Exchange (imprint as the OCR reads it)
Publication Date: 1676 (host catalogue date; the OCR of the title page does not show the year)
Rights: Public Domain (a seventeenth-century English print; public domain by date)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_early-english-books-1641-1700_the-confession-or-declar_1676, file bim_early-english-books-1641-1700_the-confession-or-declar_1676_djvu.txt, fetched directly (curl, raw bytes) by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for the-remonstrants-and-dort), 2026-10-23; host rights statement: none on the item (no possible-copyright-status and no licence field; an EEBO microfilm scan contributed by Internet Archive, not access-restricted); collections pub_early-english-books-1641-1700, bim_microfilm.
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (7113 occurrences). Rights basis restated: a seventeenth-century English print; public domain by date.
Content note: The English translation, made by an unnamed hand, of the confession the Remonstrant Brotherhood published in Dutch in 1621 and in Latin soon after; the title page says it was printed twice in Dutch and twice in Latin before this translation. Twenty-five chapters, starting with Holy Scripture and its authority (the contents run from file line 110; chapter 25 is at about line 13837, and the last chapter deals with the orders of ministers); chapter 14 is Of Prayer and Thanksgiving (line 198 in the contents). The Latin text in the corpus (remonstrants_confessio-sive-declaratio-lat_1702.txt) is a later London printing of the same confession; chapter 14 (prayer and thanksgiving) is at file line 8974 there. This is the English beside it. EEBO microfilm OCR with long s: 7113 occurrences of the long-s glyph normalised to s. OCR sampled at 10/30/50/70/90 percent; about 0.87 of tokens read as words at the five points (clean English baselines read 0.92), with scattered garble, stray marginal marks for scripture references and some lost lines; the title-page block and the contents carry heavy noise. SECOND WITNESS for exact wording: locate passages here and verify against the page images before quoting.

all, or atleast of the chief Articles. of 
Christian Religion, whereby Christian 
Reader, whoever you are,you may eafily

ourAdversaries {landerously fastened up-

as have been condemned by the ancient 
and first Professors of Christianity : alsa 
that we do not shake, nor subvert - those 
things, which have been at any time e- 
stablithed by the Church of Christ by uni- 
ver: consent : that we do not define, 
nor obstinately and proudly decide. in 
those things, which have long since been 
dubiously controverted to and fro, and 
which make not greatly either for the 
roomy of the Glory of God, or of 
our own Salvation or our Neighbour's:fi- 
nally that we do not operously & subtilly 
seatch into those things,which are not re- 
ycaledtous : lest indeed we intrude our
